尼塞韦单抗的RSV保护作用在第二年减弱

Nirsevimab's RSV Protection Fades by Second Year
新闻简报发表于《美国医学会杂志·儿科学》的一项全国性尼塞韦单抗研究发现,用于预防婴儿呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)疾病的单克隆抗体尼塞韦单抗(Beyfortus)在法国两次免疫活动的首年随访中,对RSV下呼吸道感染相关住院具有显著保护效果。然而,其保护作用在第二年减弱,且免疫接种与住院率降低之间未观察到显著关联。该研究分析了2023年和2024年尼塞韦单抗接种队列中的74,672名和175,526名婴儿,指出其有效性在不同RSV株(包括RSV A和RSV B)间保持一致。第二年研究发现,接种婴儿的耳鼻喉细菌感染和哮喘住院率较高,但这些关联基于少数事件。作者认为尼塞韦单抗的保护窗口与其药代动力学半衰期相关,而非持久免疫,并提醒哮喘信号仍具推测性。研究结果强调,在考虑为进入第二个RSV流行季的婴儿制定给药策略时,应重视该药物在首年降低住院风险的核心获益。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年8月10日发表,基于法国两次免疫活动的数据,随访期覆盖尼塞韦单抗接种后的首年和第二年。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于法国本土出生的婴儿,涉及两次尼塞韦单抗接种队列:2023年接种队列74,672名婴儿,2024年接种队列175,526名婴儿。
事件
发表于《美国医学会杂志·儿科学》的一项全国性尼塞韦单抗研究发现,尼塞韦单抗对RSV下呼吸道感染相关住院的保护作用在第二年随访中减弱,但在两次免疫活动的首年显示出显著效果。
规模、影响与风险
在首个年度(2023-2024),尼塞韦单抗使呼吸道合胞病毒下呼吸道感染相关住院率降低66%(95%置信区间,61%-70%);在2024-2025年度降低72%(95%置信区间,69%-75%)。在第二年,未观察到显著的保护关联(加权风险比,1.03;95%置信区间,0.73-1.43)。研究还指出,接种免疫的婴儿中耳鼻喉细菌感染和哮喘住院率有所上升。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究基于法国国家健康数据系统(SNDS)及配对尼塞韦单抗接种数据。作者强调,耳鼻喉和哮喘关联基于少数事件,应与RSV下呼吸道感染相关住院的显著降低相权衡。研究局限性包括需进一步调查哮喘信号及尼塞韦单抗的药代动力学半衰期。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nirsevimab's RSV Protection Fades by Second Year

News Brief
A nationwide cohort study published in JAMA Pediatrics found that nirsevimab (Beyfortus), a monoclonal antibody used to prevent RSV disease in infants, showed strong effectiveness against RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations during the first year of follow-up across two immunization campaigns in France. However, this protective effect faded in the second year, with no significant association observed between immunization and reduced hospitalizations. The study, which analyzed 74,672 and 175,526 infants in the 2023 and 2024 cohorts respectively, noted that the effectiveness was consistent across different RSV strains, including RSV A and RSV B. During the second year, the study found higher rates of hospitalization for ENT bacterial infections and asthma among immunized infants, though these associations were based on a small number of events. The authors suggested that the protective window of nirsevimab is tied to its pharmacokinetic half-life rather than lasting immunity, and they cautioned that the asthma signal remains speculative. The findings highlight the importance of considering dosing strategies for infants entering their second RSV season, as the drug's core benefit in reducing hospitalization risk during the first year remains well established.
Original source text (English)
A nationwide cohort study published in JAMA Pediatrics found that nirsevimab (Beyfortus), a monoclonal antibody used to prevent respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) disease in infants, maintained strong effectiveness against RSV-LRTI–related hospitalization across 2 consecutive French immunization campaigns but showed no protective association during a second year of follow-up. The study found higher rates of hospitalization for ear, nose, and throat (ENT) bacterial infections and asthma among immunized infants. 1 Strong first-season effectiveness against respiratory syncytial virus hospitalization but finds no protective benefit in year 2. Image Credit: Peter Hansen - stock.adobe.com Investigators analyzed 2 nationwide matched cohorts using the French National Health Data System (SNDS), following infants born in metropolitan France before the 2023 and 2024 nirsevimab immunization campaigns. Infants who received nirsevimab were matched 1:1 to unimmunized infants based on sex, birth month, gestational age, department of residence, and social deprivation index. The 2023 cohort included 74,672 infants, and the 2024 cohort included 175,526 infants. Both cohorts were followed for 1 year, with the 2023 cohort followed for an additional second year. 1 First-Year Effectiveness Held Steady Across 2 Seasons With Different RSV Strains During the first year of follow-up, RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations occurred in 0.8% of immunized versus 2.4% of unimmunized infants in the 2023 cohort and in 0.5% versus 1.9%, respectively, in the 2024 cohort. This translated to effectiveness estimates of 66% (95% CI, 61%-70%) in 2023-2024 and 72% (95% CI, 69%-75%) in 2024-2025. 1 The consistency across seasons was notable given that RSV A predominated during the 2023-2024 introduction season, and RSV B predominated during 2024-2025, alongside the emergence of RSV B variants carrying resistance-associated mutations. The researchers noted this stability was reassuring given the change in circulating RSV type and the sharp rise in the proportion of infants immunized, from about 13% in 2023-2024 to about 47% in 2024-2025. 1 In the 2024 cohort, the protective association was stronger among infants aged 3 months or younger (weighted hazard ratio [wHR], 0.22; 95% CI, 0.18-0.26) compared with those older than 3 months (wHR, 0.34; 95% CI, 0.30-0.39). 1 No Protection Seen in Second Year, With Signals for ENT Infections and Asthma During the second year of follow-up in the 2023 cohort, RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations occurred in 0.2% of both immunized and unimmunized infants, with no significant protective association observed (wHR, 1.03; 95% CI, 0.73-1.43). The authors noted this finding is biologically plausible given that nirsevimab provides passive immunity for approximately 5 months, corresponding to a single RSV season. 1 Across both years of follow-up, nirsevimab exposure was associated with higher rates of hospitalization for ENT bacterial infections, primarily acute otitis media. In the first year, this association held in both the 2023 cohort (wHR, 1.36; 95% CI, 1.00-1.84) and the 2024 cohort (wHR, 1.43; 95% CI, 1.15-1.78). During the second year, the association strengthened further (wHR, 1.56; 95% CI, 1.10-2.22). An association with higher rates of asthma hospitalization also emerged during the second year (wHR, 1.24; 95% CI, 1.05-1.46), though no such association was seen in either cohort's first year. 1 The study authors offered several possible explanations for the asthma signal, including a rebound effect after first-year protection, reduced natural immune priming during the period of passive antibody protection, and the established link between viral respiratory infections and asthma exacerbations at this age. However, they cautioned that these hypotheses "remain challenging and speculative" and that asthma diagnoses before age 2 years can be difficult to interpret, since early wheezing episodes do not always reflect persistent asthma. 1 Importantly, the authors emphasized that the ENT and asthma associations were based on a comparatively small number of events and should be weighed against the substantial reduction in RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations achieved during the first year. 1 What This Means for Pharmacists Nirsevimab (Beyfortus) is administered as a single intramuscular injection, typically given by or under the supervision of a physician shortly before or during RSV season, which generally runs from October through the end of March in most of the continental United States. 2,3 Pharmacists counseling caregivers on immunization schedules should be aware that current dosing calls for only 1 dose per season and that infants and young children at increased risk for severe RSV disease entering a second RSV season may require a separate, higher dose. 3 The new French data reinforce that nirsevimab's protective window is tied to its pharmacokinetic half-life rather than lasting immunity, a distinction that matters for counseling families of infants approaching their second RSV season. The findings on ENT infections and asthma, while modest in magnitude, may prompt questions from parents. Pharmacists can point to the study's own caveats about small event numbers and remind families that the drug's core benefit—a substantial reduction in RSV hospitalization risk during the first year—remains well established across multiple studies, including the original randomized MELODY trial. 4 Pharmacists should also remain familiar with nirsevimab's known adverse effect profile, including injection-site reactions and, in the MELODY trial, a case of nirsevimab-related generalized macular rash; serious hypersensitivity reactions, including anaphylaxis, have been described with other human IgG1 monoclonal antibodies and warrant caution in infants with bleeding disorders. 2,4 The FDA approved nirsevimab (Beyfortus, AstraZeneca) in July 2023 for the prevention of RSV lower respiratory tract disease in neonates and infants born during or entering their first RSV season and in children up to 24 months of age who remain vulnerable through their second RSV season. The approval was supported by 3 clinical trials. In the pivotal MELODY trial of term and late-preterm infants, nirsevimab reduced the risk of medically attended RSV-associated LRTI by approximately 75% relative to placebo (12 of 994 infants receiving nirsevimab experienced the outcome, compared with 25 of 496 infants receiving placebo). 3,4 REFERENCES 1. Gabet A, Kolla E, Tréluyer L, et al. First- and Second-Year Outcomes After Nirsevimab Immunization. JAMA Pediatr. Published online August 10, 2026. doi:10.1001/jamapediatrics.2026.3384 2. Jones JM, Fleming-Dutra KE, Prill MM, et al. Use of nirsevimab for the prevention of respiratory syncytial virus disease among infants and young children: recommendations of the Advisory Committee on Immunization Practices — United States, 2023. MMWR Morb Mortal Wkly Rep . 2023;72(34):920-925. doi:10.15585/mmwr.mm7234a4 3. FDA approves new drug to prevent RSV in babies and toddlers. News release. US Food and Drug Administration. July 17, 2023. Accessed August 11, 2026. https://www.fda.gov/news-events/press-announcements/fda-approves-new-drug-prevent-rsv-babies-and-toddlers 4. Hammitt LL, Dagan R, Yuan Y, et al. Nirsevimab for Prevention of RSV in Healthy Late-Preterm and Term Infants. N Engl J Med . 2022;386(9):837-846. doi:10.1056/NEJMoa2110275
View five news elements
Time
The study was published on August 10, 2026, and the findings were based on data from two immunization campaigns in France, with follow-up periods spanning the first and second years post-vaccination.
Location and population
The study focused on infants born in metropolitan France, with two cohorts: 74,672 infants in the 2023 cohort and 175,526 infants in the 2024 cohort.
Event
A nationwide cohort study published in JAMA Pediatrics found that nirsevimab's protective effect against RSV-LRTI-related hospitalization faded in the second year of follow-up, but it showed strong effectiveness during the first year across two immunization campaigns.
Scale, impact and risk
During the first year, nirsevimab reduced RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations by 66% (95% CI, 61%-70%) in 2023-2024 and 72% (95% CI, 69%-75%) in 2024-2025. In the second year, there was no significant protective association observed (wHR, 1.03; 95% CI, 0.73-1.43). The study also noted higher rates of ENT bacterial infections and asthma hospitalizations among immunized infants.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study's findings are based on the French National Health Data System (SNDS) and matched cohorts. The authors emphasized that the ENT and asthma associations were based on a small number of events and should be weighed against the substantial reduction in RSV-LRTI-related hospitalizations. The study's limitations include the need for further investigation into the asthma signal and the pharmacokinetic half-life of nirsevimab.

Australian Data Put the Maternal RSV Vaccine at 78 Percent Effective Against Infant Lung Infection Admissions - Medical Daily

Australian Data Put the Maternal RSV Vaccine at 78 Percent Effective Against Infant Lung Infection Admissions - Medical Daily
新闻简报A new national study in Australia estimates that maternal RSV vaccination during pregnancy is 77.8 percent effective at preventing infant hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age. The findings, published in JAMA Pediatrics and led by researchers at The Kids Research Institute Australia, are based on real-world data from 1,012 infants hospitalized with acute respiratory infection across nine Australian hospitals between March 2025 and February 2026. The study used a test-negative case-control design and found that 32 percent of infants born to unvaccinated mothers developed lower respiratory tract disease, compared with 17 percent of those whose mothers were vaccinated. The effectiveness was also reported as 80.4 percent against severe lower respiratory tract disease and 80.8 percent against RSV-associated acute respiratory illness. The results highlight the potential protective benefits of maternal vaccination against RSV-related hospitalizations in infants, though the study does not measure whether vaccinated mothers' babies avoided RSV altogether. The findings are relevant for expectant parents in the United States considering how to protect their babies born this fall.

地点与对象
澳大利亚;6月龄及以下因急性呼吸道感染住院的婴儿,研究覆盖澳大利亚9家医院。
事件
A study published in JAMA Pediatrics found that maternal RSV vaccination was 77.8 percent effective at preventing infant hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age. The study was led by researchers at The Kids Research Institute Australia.
规模、影响与风险
The maternal RSV vaccine was found to be 77.8 percent effective against hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age. It was 80.4 percent effective against hospitalization for severe lower respiratory tract disease and 80.8 percent effective against hospitalization for RSV-associated acute respiratory illness. Among all infants hospitalized with respiratory illness in the study, 32 percent of those born to unvaccinated mothers had developed lower respiratory tract disease, compared with 17 percent of those whose mothers were vaccinated.
应对、状态与不确定性
The study used a test-negative case-control design and was published in JAMA Pediatrics. The findings are based on real-world data and are not a randomized trial. The effectiveness of the maternal RSV vaccine is reported as 77.8 percent against hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age.

Innovation Australian Data Put the Maternal RSV Vaccine at 78 Percent Effective Against Infant Lung Infection Admissions Published Aug 21, 2026 8:57 AM EDT By Elena Vega RAUL ARBOLEDA | AFP via Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on Pocket A new national study estimates that RSV vaccination during pregnancy was 77.8 percent effective at preventing infant hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age, a longer protection window than most previously published real-world estimates have measured. The findings come from the STREETON study, published in JAMA Pediatrics and led by researchers at The Kids Research Institute Australia. It analyzed 1,012 infants six months or younger who were hospitalized with acute respiratory infection across nine Australian hospitals between March 2025 and February 2026. For expectant parents in the United States weighing how to protect a baby born this fall, the useful part is not the headline percentage. It is the endpoint that was actually measured, and the age range it covers, because those two details determine whether the number applies to the decision in front of them. The Endpoint That Was Actually Measured Lower respiratory tract disease is the clinical category that includes bronchiolitis and pneumonia, the conditions that put infants on oxygen. It is not the same as any RSV infection, and the study did not measure whether vaccinated mothers' babies avoided RSV altogether. The estimates, drawn from a test-negative case-control design, break out as follows. Vaccination was 77.8 percent effective against hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months, 80.4 percent effective against hospitalization for severe lower respiratory tract disease, and 80.8 percent effective against hospitalization for RSV-associated acute respiratory illness. Among all infants hospitalized with respiratory illness in the study, 32 percent of those born to unvaccinated mothers had developed lower respiratory tract disease, compared with 17 percent of those whose mothers were vaccinated. Maternal vaccination was defined as receipt of RSVpreF at least 14 days before delivery. Protection Runs Highest in the First Eight Weeks The age breakdown is the finding with the most practical weight. Among infants two months and younger, effectiveness against lower respiratory tract disease hospitalization was 86.3 percent. From birth through three months it was 80.2 percent, easing toward the 77.8 percent figure across the full six-month window. That gradient aligns with where the danger sits. Most RSV hospitalizations occur in the first three months of life, when airways are smallest, and the risk of severe outcomes is highest. RSV is a leading cause of lower respiratory tract disease in infants worldwide. The pattern of higher early protection that eases over six months is expected. Maternal vaccination works by transferring antibodies across the placenta, and those antibodies wane in the infant over time. A single number covering six months therefore understates protection during the weeks when it matters most and overstates it later. Dr. Ushma Wadia, a clinician-scientist at the Wesfarmers Centre of Vaccines and Infectious Diseases and a pediatrician at Perth Children's Hospital, said the results represent "some of the strongest real-world evidence demonstrating that maternal RSV vaccination is working" in Australia. Timing Flexibility Inside the Recommended Window One secondary result carries practical value for scheduling. Effectiveness remained similar regardless of when in the recommended window the vaccine was given, how long the interval was between vaccination and delivery, and whether RSVpreF was administered near the time of another vaccine. That last point addresses a common source of hesitation. Pregnant patients often ask whether combining the RSV vaccine with other routine maternal vaccines at the same visit reduces its effect. In this analysis, it did not, which supports giving it during routine antenatal appointments rather than scheduling an extra visit. Two caveats accompany the finding. Australia's program funds maternal RSV vaccination from 28 weeks of gestation, while the U.S. label specifies 32 through 36 weeks. Australia also offers the vaccine year-round because RSV circulates year-round in the north and seasonally in the south, whereas the United States has a defined seasonal window. A pregnant patient in Chicago or Atlanta should follow U.S. guidance and their obstetric provider's recommendation, not the Australian schedule. Applying an Australian Result to a U.S. Season This is an observational study, not a randomized trial. A test-negative design compares vaccination rates between infants who tested positive for RSV and those who did not, and it can be affected by unmeasured differences between families who vaccinate and those who do not. The estimates are effectiveness figures, not proof of a causal effect in any individual child. The study was conducted within Australia's RSV Maternal and Infant Protection Program, which pairs maternal vaccination with jurisdiction-funded nirsevimab for infants. Disentangling one product's contribution inside a hybrid program is genuinely difficult. STREETON is also registered as a Pfizer-sponsored trial, and Pfizer manufactures RSVpreF. Independent peer review does not remove sponsorship, and readers should weigh it. Researchers said they will continue evaluating effectiveness over time with larger samples, including in infant subgroups and beyond six months. The findings are consistent with, rather than a departure from, U.S. evidence. A University of Pittsburgh and UPMC analysis published in JAMA Network Open in June linked maternal vaccination to fewer RSV-related hospitalizations among infants 90 days and younger. Current U.S. recommendations do not change based on the Australian study, and an independent summary of the effectiveness estimates reached the same conclusion. MedicalDaily reported last week that expectant parents have two ways to protect a newborn before the season opens: the maternal vaccine during pregnancy, or a long-acting monoclonal antibody given to the baby after birth. For most infants, only one is needed, and the choice usually comes down to timing and access. Pregnant patients should raise the timing question at their next prenatal visit, and parents of newborns should ask their pediatrician whether the infant antibody is indicated if the maternal dose was missed or given fewer than 14 days before delivery. Infants with trouble breathing, rapid or labored breathing, poor feeding, fewer wet diapers, or bluish coloring around the lips need urgent evaluation, regardless of what protection they received. Read more Vaccines Given During Pregnancy Cover a Newborn's First Months Before Any Infant Shots Begin Preventing RSV in Infants Also Reduced Antibiotic Prescribing Across 32 Philadelphia Area Pediatric Primary Care Practices Expectant Parents Have Two Ways to Shield a Newborn from RSV Before the Season Opens in the Fall Key Questions Answered What did the study find? Maternal RSV vaccination was estimated at 77.8 percent effective against infant hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age. What kind of study was it? A test-negative case-control study of 1,012 hospitalized infants at nine Australian hospitals, published in JAMA Pediatrics. It is observational, not a randomized trial. Does it mean my baby cannot get RSV? No. The study measured hospitalization for lower respiratory tract disease, not infection overall. When is protection strongest? In the first two months of life, where effectiveness against lower respiratory tract disease hospitalization was 86.3 percent. Does timing within the window matter? Effectiveness was similar regardless of when the vaccine was given inside the recommended window or whether it was given alongside another vaccine. Does the U.S. schedule match Australia's? No. Australia funds it from 28 weeks year-round. The U.S. label specifies 32 through 36 weeks, and STREETON is a Pfizer-sponsored study of the company's own vaccine. When should a parent seek urgent care? Trouble breathing, rapid or labored breathing, poor feeding, fewer wet diapers or bluish coloring around the lips. Aug 25, 03:19 pm EDT Medical Daily VITALITY MENTAL HEALTH INNOVATION HEALTHY LIVING Wellness Awards CONDITIONS Search form Search Vitality How to Live Better, Longer Firefighters Union Warns Members Against Glutathione IV and Nebulizer Treatments Offered at Wildfire Camps This Summer The IAFF warns wildland firefighters that glutathione given by IV or nebulizer is unproven, after two pharmacies recalled contaminated batches.
Location and population
Australia, infants six months or younger hospitalized with acute respiratory infection across nine Australian hospitals.
Event
A study published in JAMA Pediatrics found that maternal RSV vaccination was 77.8 percent effective at preventing infant hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age. The study was led by researchers at The Kids Research Institute Australia.
Scale, impact and risk
The maternal RSV vaccine was found to be 77.8 percent effective against hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age. It was 80.4 percent effective against hospitalization for severe lower respiratory tract disease and 80.8 percent effective against hospitalization for RSV-associated acute respiratory illness. Among all infants hospitalized with respiratory illness in the study, 32 percent of those born to unvaccinated mothers had developed lower respiratory tract disease, compared with 17 percent of those whose mothers were vaccinated.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study used a test-negative case-control design and was published in JAMA Pediatrics. The findings are based on real-world data and are not a randomized trial. The effectiveness of the maternal RSV vaccine is reported as 77.8 percent against hospitalization for RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease through six months of age.

尼塞韦单抗(尼塞韦单抗)对呼吸道合胞病毒的保护作用在第二年减弱——《药物话题》

Nirsevimab's RSV Protection Fades by Second Year - Drug Topics
新闻简报《药物话题》于2026年8月25日发布的文章讨论了尼塞韦单抗(一种用于预防呼吸道合胞病毒的药物)的保护效果随时间推移而减弱的问题。文章指出,尼塞韦单抗提供的保护作用可能在第二年减退,从而引发对其长期有效性的担忧。该文强调了持续监测该药物性能的重要性,并需考虑其在呼吸道合胞病毒预防中的作用。然而,文章未提供具体病例或死亡人数,也未详述任何官方回应或进一步调查。文章重点关注此发现对公共卫生和临床实践的影响,特别是在呼吸道合胞病毒季节规划和呼吸道合胞病毒疫苗接种政策调整的背景下。该文是更广泛讨论药物进展及其对患者护理影响的一部分。

事件
《药物话题》报道称,尼塞韦单抗对呼吸道合胞病毒的保护作用在第二年减弱。文章讨论了该药物效果随时间推移而减弱的问题。
规模、影响与风险
文章强调尼塞韦单抗在预防呼吸道合胞病毒感染效果随时间减弱的潜在风险,但未提供具体病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
文章基于研究和临床观察报告尼塞韦单抗的有效性可能在第一年后下降。文章未提及卫生当局采取的具体行动或进一步调查。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nirsevimab's RSV Protection Fades by Second Year - Drug Topics

News Brief
The article published on August 25, 2026, by Drug Topics discusses the diminishing effectiveness of Nirsevimab, a drug used to protect against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), over time. It notes that the protection provided by Nirsevimab may fade by the second year, raising concerns about its long-term efficacy. The piece highlights the importance of monitoring the drug's performance and considering its role in RSV prevention. However, it does not provide specific case or death counts, nor does it detail any official responses or further investigations. The focus is on the implications of this finding for public health and clinical practice, particularly in the context of RSV season planning and vaccine policy changes. The article is part of a broader discussion on pharmaceutical advancements and their impact on patient care.

孕妇呼吸道合胞病毒疫苗接种高效预防婴儿住院 - CIDRAP

Maternal RSV vaccination highly effective against infant hospitalization - CIDRAP
新闻简报澳大利亚一项新研究于2026年8月20日发表于《JAMA 儿科学》杂志,发现孕期接种呼吸道合胞病毒疫苗高效预防6个月及以下婴儿因呼吸道合胞病毒相关住院。该疫苗接种项目(疫苗接种)于2025年2月启动,鼓励孕妇在妊娠28周后接种二价呼吸道合胞病毒前体蛋白疫苗(RSVpreF,商品名Abrysvo)。研究分析了2025年3月至2026年2月间在澳大利亚9家医院因急性呼吸道感染住院的1000余名婴儿的数据。研究者将孕妇疫苗接种定义为在分娩前至少14天接种RSVpreF疫苗。该疫苗对2个月及以下婴儿呼吸道合胞病毒相关住院的有效性为86.3%,对6个月内任何呼吸道合胞病毒相关下呼吸道疾病(LRTD)住院的有效性为77.8%。在因呼吸系统疾病住院的婴儿中,母未接种疫苗者中32%发展为LRTD,而母已接种疫苗者中这一比例为17%。研究结果表明,孕妇免疫接种是澳大利亚呼吸道合胞病毒母婴保护计划的核心组成部分,且在生命早期高风险月份中保护效果最为显著。研究人员强调需持续评估该疫苗效果随时间的变化。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2025年3月至2026年2月进行,呼吸道合胞病毒孕妇疫苗接种项目(疫苗接种)于2025年2月启动。研究结果于2026年8月20日发表。
地点与对象
该研究聚焦于澳大利亚6个月及以下婴儿,具体为2025年母婴呼吸道合胞病毒疫苗接种项目实施首年在9家医院因急性呼吸道感染住院的婴儿人群。
事件
《JAMA 儿科学》杂志发表的一项研究发现,孕期接种呼吸道合胞病毒疫苗高效预防6个月及以下婴儿因呼吸道合胞病毒相关住院,且在生命早期高风险月份中保护效果最为显著。该研究由西澳医疗中心呼吸道合胞病毒与感染性疾病研究所及澳大利亚儿童研究所的研究人员共同开展。
规模、影响与风险
该疫苗对2个月及以下婴儿呼吸道合胞病毒相关住院的有效性为86.3%,对6个月内任何呼吸道合胞病毒相关下呼吸道疾病住院的有效性为77.8%。在因呼吸系统疾病住院的婴儿中,母未接种疫苗者中32%发展为下呼吸道疾病,而母已接种疫苗者中这一比例为17%。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究结果支持孕妇免疫接种作为澳大利亚呼吸道合胞病毒母婴保护计划核心组成部分的有效性。研究人员呼吁持续评估该疫苗效果随时间的变化。该项目于2025年2月启动,因呼吸道合胞病毒流行模式差异,在澳大利亚全年提供接种服务。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Maternal RSV vaccination highly effective against infant hospitalization - CIDRAP

News Brief
A new study from Australia, published in JAMA Pediatrics on August 20, 2026, found that maternal RSV vaccination during pregnancy is highly effective in preventing RSV-related hospitalizations in infants aged 6 months or younger. The vaccination program, introduced in February 2025, encourages pregnant women to receive the bivalent RSVpreF vaccine (Abrysvo) from 28 weeks’ gestation. The study analyzed data from over 1,000 infants hospitalized with acute respiratory infection (ARI) at nine Australian hospitals between March 2025 and February 2026. Researchers defined maternal vaccination as receiving the RSVpreF vaccine at least 14 days before delivery. The vaccine showed 86.3% effectiveness against RSV-related hospitalizations in infants 2 months and younger, and 77.8% effectiveness against any RSV-associated lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD) through 6 months of age. Among infants hospitalized with respiratory illness, 32% of those born to unvaccinated mothers had developed LRTD, compared with 17% of those whose mothers had been vaccinated. The findings suggest that maternal immunization is a key component of Australia’s RSV Maternal and Infant Protection Program, with the greatest benefit observed during the earliest, highest-risk months of life. Researchers emphasize the need for ongoing evaluation to monitor vaccine effectiveness over time.
Original source text (English)

Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search Support Menu Maternal RSV vaccination highly effective against infant hospitalization Laine Bergeson August 20, 2026 Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV) Anastasiia Havrysh / iStock Share Vaccination against respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) during pregnancy is highly effective in preventing RSV-related hospitalization in infants aged 6 months or younger, according to a new study from Australia, with protection strongest during the first months of life. Australia introduced a maternal RSV vaccination program in February 2025 to encourage pregnant women to receive the bivalent (two-strain) RSVpreF vaccine (Abrysvo) from 28 weeks’ gestation. The study findings, published this week in JAMA Pediatrics, draw on data from more than 1,000 infants at nine hospitals born during the first year of the vaccination program. RSV is the leading cause of lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD) in infants globally, with most hospitalizations occurring during the first three months of life, when the most severe outcomes often occur. Australia offers the vaccine year-round, because RSV circulation varies across the country, with seasonal peaks in the southern states and year-round activity in the north. Protection strongest in first 2 months of life For the study, researchers from Wesfarmers Centre for Vaccines and Infectious Diseases at the Kids Research Institute Australia analyzed 1,012 infants 6 months or younger who were hospitalized with acute respiratory infection (ARI) from March 2025 to February 2026. Maternal vaccination was defined as receipt of RSVpreF at least 14 days before delivery. Protection was highest early in life: Among infants 2 months and younger, vaccine effectiveness was 86.3%. The researchers found that maternal vaccination was 77.8% effective against hospitalization for any RSV-associated LRTD through 6 months of age. Protection was highest early in life: Among infants 2 months and younger, vaccine effectiveness (VE) was 86.3%. From birth through 3 months, VE was 80.2%. Comparable VE was observed for hospitalization for severe RSV-related LRTD (80.4%) and RSV-associated ARI (80.8%). Among all infants hospitalized with respiratory illness, 32% of those born to unvaccinated mothers had developed LRTD, compared with 17% of those whose mothers had been vaccinated. Protection consistent, regardless of timing Effectiveness remained similar, regardless of when pregnant women were vaccinated during the recommended window, the length of interval between vaccination and delivery, or whether RSVpreF was given near the time of another vaccine. The vaccine demonstrated protection against both the RSV-A strain and RSV-B. The findings are consistent with studies in countries, such as the United States, that use RSVpreF as their primary immunization strategy or as part of hybrid prevention strategies, write the researchers. While they call for ongoing evaluation to monitor VE over time, they conclude that their findings “support the effectiveness of maternal immunization as a key component of Australia’s RSV Maternal and Infant Protection Program, with the greatest benefit observed during the earliest, highest-risk months of life.” Related news Previous Next Asthma, chronic kidney disease and other high-risk 流感 vs. 感冒 vs. 呼吸道合胞病毒 - UT Health East Texas

Flu vs. Cold vs. RSV - UT Health East Texas
新闻简报UT Health East Texas 于 2026 年 8 月 20 日发表的文章比较了 呼吸道合胞病毒 (RSV)、流感和感冒的症状和预防方法。它强调,这些疾病虽然具有相似的症状,但可以通过特定的体征来区分。这篇文章强调了了解这些差异以做出明智的健康决策并保护自己和他人的重要性。它提供了管理症状的一般指导,例如休息、补水和非处方药,并建议严重病例寻求医疗护理。该文章还提倡使用 疫苗接种,就像流感疫苗一样,作为预防措施。没有报告具体的 暴发 数据或风险级别,重点是公众健康意识而不是已确认的事件。
查看新闻五要素
时间
感冒、流感和呼吸道合胞病毒在寒冷的月份尤其普遍。
地点与对象
UT Health 德克萨斯州东部,一般人群。
事件
UT Health East Texas 发布了一篇比较呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)、流感及普通感冒症状与预防方法的文章。该文章讨论了这些疾病之间的差异,并强调了解它们的重要性,以便做出明智的健康决策。
规模、影响与风险
该文章未报告具体病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。其重点在于这些疾病及其症状的一般信息,未提及任何呼吸道合胞病毒或风险等级。
应对、状态与不确定性
该文章提供了通过卫生措施、抗病毒药物及必要时寻求医疗护理来管理症状和预防疾病的一般指导。未报告与呼吸道合胞病毒相关的任何具体调查、检测或隔离措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Flu vs. Cold vs. RSV - UT Health East Texas

News Brief
The article published by UT Health East Texas on August 20, 2026, compares the symptoms and prevention methods of respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), the flu, and colds. It highlights that these illnesses, while having similar symptoms, can be distinguished by specific signs. The piece emphasizes the importance of understanding these differences to make informed health decisions and protect oneself and others. It provides general guidance on managing symptoms, such as rest, hydration, and over-the-counter medications, and advises seeking medical care for severe cases. The article also promotes the use of vaccinations, like the flu shot, as a preventive measure. No specific outbreak data or risk levels are reported, and the focus is on public health awareness rather than a confirmed event.
Original source text (English)
Skip to content Contact Us Menu Online Bill Pay MyChart About Careers Price Transparency Contact Us Find a Provider Services Allergy Treatment Breast Health Cardiology Cosmetic and Plastic Surgery Ear, Nose and Throat Emergency Care Endocrinology Family Medicine Gastroenterology Gynecologic Center All Services Patients & Visitors Locations UT Health Athens UT Health Carthage UT Health East Texas Hope Cancer Center UT Health East Texas Physicians UT Health East Texas Rehabilitation Center UT Health Henderson UT Health Jacksonville UT Health North Campus Tyler UT Health Pittsburg UT Health Quitman UT Health Tyler UT Health Tyler Bariatric Center All Locations Home » Flu vs. Cold vs. RSV Flu vs. Cold vs. RSV August 20, 2026 Health symptoms, treatment When you or a loved one starts to cough or have a runny nose, it’s normal to worry – especially in the fall and winter when everyone seems to be getting sick. Colds, the flu and RSV are especially widespread during the colder months. Though these illnesses have similar symptoms, there are a few telltale signs that can help you tell them apart. By understanding each condition, you can learn how to protect yourself from getting sick, how to treat the symptoms and know when you need professional medical care. What is a Cold? Rhinovirus, or the common cold, can be spread and caught year-round, but it’s particularly pervasive during fall and winter. Colds are generally mild and come on gradually. Symptoms include: Runny nose Sore throat Cough Sneezing Watery eyes Little to no fever How is it Treated? Most colds improve on their own within seven to 10 days. They can be managed with rest, hydration, and over-the-counter medications like Tylenol and Ibuprofen. Sometimes coughing and congestion can last longer than 10 days. Call your doctor if you’re experiencing more serious breathing complications. What is the Flu? The flu is a highly contagious respiratory illness caused by the influenza virus. It infects tissues in the nose and throat, and sometimes the lungs. It causes mild to severe illness, and typically flu symptoms appear suddenly, within one to four days. Common symptoms of the flu include: Runny nose Fatigue Cough Headache Body aches Fever or chills Some people may experience more severe flu symptoms. If you experience any of the following symptoms, seek immediate medical attention: Difficulty breathing Persistent chest or stomach pain Confusion or dizziness Severe weakness or muscle pain Inability to urinate Seizures How is it Treated? The flu can be treated with rest, hydration and antiviral medications prescribed by a doctor. If you get sick with the flu, you should: Stay home until you feel better Drink plenty of fluids, such as water and soup Avoid things that could worsen your symptoms, such as alcohol or tobacco Take ibuprofen or Tylenol to help reduce your fever One of the best ways to protect yourself and others against the flu is by getting a flu shot. Many pharmacies and clinics offer the flu shot for free each year, and the vaccine protects against multiple strains of the flu – not just one. Yearly flu vaccination is a simple, science‐backed step you can take toward staying healthy and preventing the spread of illness, especially to those who are most vulnerable. What is RSV? Respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is a common respiratory virus that infects the nose, throat, lungs and respiratory tract. It is contagious and easily spread through close contact with someone who has RSV, coughing and sneezing, and contaminated surfaces. Common symptoms of RSV include: Runny nose or congestion Decreased appetite Cough Sneezing Wheezing Fever Symptoms of RSV in babies and children may present differently. Go to an emergency room if your child has severe RSV symptoms, which include: Shortness of breath, wheezing or noisy breathing Bluish or grayish skin, nails and lips Nostrils flaring when breathing Short, shallow or fast breathing Pauses in breathing Caving in of the chest with expansion of the belly when breathing How is it Treated? Though there is no specific treatment for RSV, treating the symptoms may help you feel better sooner. RSV symptoms can be treated in the following ways: Taking medications, such as over-the-counter pain relievers or fever reducers Drinking fluids and staying hydrated Talk to your doctor if your symptoms worsen. Staying Healthy While colds, the flu and RSV share many of the same symptoms, understanding the differences can help you make informed decisions about your health and know when it’s time to seek medical care. Practicing good hygiene, staying home when you’re sick, keeping up with recommended vaccinations and taking steps to support your immune system can help reduce your risk of illness. If you’re unsure about your symptoms or they become severe, don’t hesitate to contact your healthcare provider. With the right precautions and prompt care when needed, you can help protect yourself, your family and your community throughout cold and flu season. 法国 监测 分析将 RSV 季节与患病儿童肺炎球菌菌株的变化联系起来 - 医学日报

A French Surveillance Analysis Links RSV Season to a Shift in Which Pneumococcal Strains Sicken Children - Medical Daily
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 20 日发布的法国 监测 分析将 RSV 季节与导致儿童患病的肺炎球菌菌株的变化联系起来。CIDRAP 总结的这项研究分析了法国三个 监测 系统 15 年的数据,涵盖 15 岁以下儿童的侵袭性肺炎球菌疾病、肺炎球菌携带和 RSV 感染。研究发现,RSV 季节期间发生了 1,621 例侵袭性肺炎球菌疾病,而其他季节则为 2,953 例。三种血清型——24F、19A 和 1——占所有病例的 30% 以上。分析表明,某些肺炎球菌菌株在 RSV 流行期间可能会变得更具毒性,但这并不意味着 RSV 通常会导致细菌性血流感染或脑膜炎。该研究是一项机械发现,不会改变 疫苗 的建议。它强调了 RSV 和肺炎球菌菌株之间的潜在相互作用,但没有提供因果关系或儿童风险增加的直接证据。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年8月20日,美国东部时间上午8:20
地点与对象
法国,15岁以下儿童
事件
法国一项监测分析将呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)季节与导致儿童患病的肺炎链球菌菌株变化联系起来。该研究由《Medical Daily》发表,并由CIDRAP进行了摘要。
规模、影响与风险
分析发现,在呼吸道合胞病毒季节期间,侵袭性肺炎链球菌疾病病例为1,621例,而在季节外为2,953例。三种血清型(24F、19A和1型)占所有病例的30%以上。研究未表明呼吸道合胞病毒感染常导致细菌性血流感染或脑膜炎。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究为一项监测分析,不改变任何疫苗建议。这是一种机制性发现,而非预警。研究数据基于2008年至2023年。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

A French Surveillance Analysis Links RSV Season to a Shift in Which Pneumococcal Strains Sicken Children - Medical Daily

News Brief
A French surveillance analysis published on August 20, 2026, links RSV season to a shift in pneumococcal strains that sicken children. The study, summarized by CIDRAP, analyzed 15 years of data from three French surveillance systems covering invasive pneumococcal disease, pneumococcal carriage, and RSV infections in children under 15. It found that 1,621 cases of invasive pneumococcal disease occurred during RSV season, compared to 2,953 outside of it. Three serotypes—24F, 19A, and 1—accounted for over 30% of all cases. The analysis suggests that certain pneumococcal strains may become more virulent during RSV epidemics, but it does not imply that RSV commonly leads to bacterial bloodstream infections or meningitis. The study is a mechanistic finding and does not alter vaccine recommendations. It highlights a potential interaction between RSV and pneumococcal strains but does not provide direct evidence of causation or increased risk for children.

Conditions A French Surveillance Analysis Links RSV Season to a Shift in Which Pneumococcal Strains Sicken Children Published Aug 20, 2026 8:20 AM EDT By Joseph James CN-STR | AFP via Getty Images Share on Twitter Share on Facebook Share on Pocket The mix of bacterial strains that cause serious pneumococcal infections in children appears to change depending on whether respiratory syncytial virus is circulating, according to an analysis of fifteen years of French surveillance data. The finding suggests that some strains rarely capable of causing severe illness on their own become more likely to do so during RSV epidemics. The study appears in The Journal of Infectious Diseases and was summarized by CIDRAP. Researchers combined three continuous French surveillance systems covering invasive pneumococcal disease, pneumococcal carriage in the nose and throat, and RSV infections, spanning 2008 through 2023 in children younger than 15. For parents heading into the fall respiratory season, this is a mechanistic finding rather than an alert. It does not change any vaccine recommendation, and it does not mean RSV infection commonly leads to bacterial bloodstream infection or meningitis. Invasive pneumococcal disease remains uncommon. The Biology Behind the Association Streptococcus pneumoniae lives harmlessly in the nose and upper throat of many healthy children. That state is called nasopharyngeal colonization, and it is the necessary first step before the bacterium can cause invasive disease. The transition from carrying the bacterium to becoming seriously ill with it is the event researchers care about, and respiratory infection is one of the recognized conditions that can prompt it. The French team used surveillance data to ask a more specific question than whether that transition happens at all. They asked whether the particular strains involved differ depending on RSV activity. They analyzed 4,574 invasive pneumococcal disease cases across the study period. Of those, 1,621 occurred during the RSV season, and 2,953 occurred outside it. Three serotypes, designated 24F, 19A, and 1, accounted for more than 30 percent of all cases. The authors reported that the distribution of serotypes among invasive cases varied strongly depending on RSV circulation. Several serotypes, including 1, 17F, 7F, 38, 8, and 12F, were proportionally more likely to appear outside the RSV season. The inverse pattern is what carries the clinical implication: strains with lower intrinsic capacity to cause invasive disease appear more likely to do so during RSV epidemics. The researchers concluded that this serotype-specific association with RSV may have important clinical consequences and may also have important implications for RSV prevention strategies in early childhood. Reading the Evidence Honestly This is observational surveillance analysis, not a trial, and the distinction is not a technicality here. The design can show that the composition of invasive cases differs between RSV and non-RSV periods. It cannot demonstrate that RSV infection caused any individual case to become invasive. Seasonality itself is a confounder, since influenza and other respiratory viruses circulate in the same months, as do behavioral changes such as increased indoor crowding. The raw case split also invites misreading. More invasive cases occurred outside the RSV season than during it, which is unsurprising given that the non-RSV period covers more of the calendar. The analysis is about proportions within each period, not about which season carries more disease. The data are French. Pneumococcal vaccine schedules, circulating strains, and RSV prevention practices differ between countries, so the specific serotype patterns should not be assumed to transfer directly to the United States. Independent evidence points in a compatible direction without settling the question. MedicalDaily previously reported on a large French study in which babies who received an RSV antibody had fewer pneumococcal hospitalizations. That analysis was also observational, rested on a small number of events, and its authors said the finding needs confirmation. Practical Meaning for Families Before the Season Nothing here changes what parents should do, and that is worth stating plainly rather than leaving it implied. Pneumococcal conjugate vaccination on the routine childhood schedule remains the established protection against invasive pneumococcal disease. RSV prevention for infants remains recommended on its own merits for preventing RSV hospitalization. CDC's guidance on protecting infants from RSV describes the two routes: maternal vaccination during weeks 32 through 36 of pregnancy, or a long-acting antibody given to the baby. Children at higher risk of severe pneumococcal disease include infants, children with chronic heart, lung, or kidney conditions, children with weakened immune systems, and those without a functioning spleen. Families in that category should confirm with a pediatrician that all recommended doses are current before the season begins. The clinical guidance for RSV immunizations sets out which infants and young children qualify for which product. Warning signs that warrant prompt medical attention in a young child with a respiratory illness include high or persistent fever, unusual sleepiness or difficulty waking, fast or labored breathing, chest retractions, a stiff neck, a rash that does not fade under pressure, or a child who seems markedly worse after appearing to improve. Those features can indicate a bacterial complication and should be evaluated rather than watched at home. This article is general information and is not a diagnosis. The authors framed their conclusion as a question for RSV prevention strategy rather than an answer. Whether preventing RSV in infancy measurably reduces invasive pneumococcal disease is the point that further research, ideally including data from other countries, would need to establish. If that question is eventually answered in the affirmative, the practical consequence would be a stronger case for RSV prevention in infancy, not a weaker one for pneumococcal vaccination. The two protect against different things through different mechanisms, and neither substitutes for the other. Parents evaluating what to accept before the season should treat this study as background rather than as a decision point. Read more Expectant Parents Have Two Ways to Shield a Newborn from RSV Before the Season Opens in the Fall Infants Given the RSV Antibody Received Fewer Antibiotic Prescriptions, Philadelphia Study Finds Flu and COVID Vaccines Are Expected This Fall with Insurer Coverage Despite a Stalled Federal Advisory Committee Key Questions Answered What did the study actually find? That the distribution of pneumococcal serotypes causing invasive disease in French children differed depending on whether RSV was circulating, with some lower-risk serotypes appearing more likely to cause invasive disease during RSV epidemics. Does RSV cause invasive pneumococcal disease? The study cannot establish that. It is an observational surveillance analysis showing an association between RSV circulation and which serotypes appear in invasive cases. How large was the analysis? It included 4,574 invasive pneumococcal disease cases in children under 15, drawn from three French surveillance systems covering 2008 through 2023. Should parents change anything? No. Routine pneumococcal vaccination and existing RSV prevention recommendations for infants are unchanged. Which children face the highest risk of invasive pneumococcal disease? Infants, children with chronic heart, lung or kidney disease, those with weakened immune systems, and children without a functioning spleen. What symptoms should prompt urgent evaluation? High or persistent fever, unusual sleepiness, fast or labored breathing, chest retractions, stiff neck, a rash that does not fade under pressure, or sudden worsening after apparent improvement. Do these findings apply to the United States? Not directly. Vaccine schedules, circulating strains and RSV prevention practices differ by country, so the specific serotype patterns need confirmation elsewhere. 华润紫竹药业RSV重组蛋白疫苗获美国FDA批准在美国启动临床试验

CSPC Pharmaceutical's RSV Vaccine Cleared by U.S. FDA to Begin Clinical Trials in the United States - finance.biggo.com
新闻简报华润紫竹医药集团(CSPC Pharmaceutical Group)宣布,其自主研发的重组蛋白疫苗(呼吸道合胞病毒,RSV)SY56057已获美国食品药品监督管理局(FDA)批准在美国启动临床试验。这是该公司第二个获准开展临床试验的重组蛋白疫苗,标志着其疫苗研发管线已进入国际多中心开发阶段。该产品的临床适应症针对60岁及以上成人预防RSV诱发的下呼吸道疾病。临床前研究数据显示,疫苗具有良好的安全性和有效性。尽管RSV重组蛋白疫苗的临床开发周期较长,但FDA的批准是向前迈出的重要一步。SYS6057的成功将取决于后续临床试验的结果,这可能为华润紫竹的疫苗业务带来增长机遇,并巩固其在生物制剂领域的竞争地位。

实时新闻
地点与对象
美国,60岁及以上成人
事件
石药集团重组RSV 疫苗(SYS6057)获得FDA批准在美国开始临床试验。这是该公司报告的已确认事件。
规模、影响与风险
疫苗是华润紫竹第二个获准开展临床试验的重组蛋白疫苗,临床前数据显示其具有良好的安全性和有效性。该产品的临床适应症针对60岁及以上成人预防RSV诱发的下呼吸道疾病。
应对、状态与不确定性
FDA已批准疫苗开展临床试验,试验将在美国启动。RSV重组蛋白疫苗的临床开发周期较长,从I期到III期可能需要数年时间。试验结果将决定该产品在市场上的成功与否。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

CSPC Pharmaceutical's RSV Vaccine Cleared by U.S. FDA to Begin Clinical Trials in the United States - finance.biggo.com

News Brief
CSPC Pharmaceutical Group has announced that its independently developed recombinant respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccine, SYS6057, has received approval from the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) to begin clinical trials in the United States. This marks the company's second recombinant protein vaccine to obtain clinical trial clearance, indicating its vaccine R&D pipeline is entering the international multi-center development stage. The clinical indication targets adults aged 60 and older for the prevention of RSV-induced lower respiratory tract disease. Preclinical study data suggest the vaccine has favorable safety and efficacy profiles. While the clinical development cycle for RSV vaccines is lengthy, the FDA clearance is a significant step forward. The success of SYS6057 will depend on the results of subsequent clinical trials, which could open up growth opportunities for CSPC's vaccine business and strengthen its competitive position in the biologics sector.

Ask AgentReal-time News Market Earnings Call More Earnings CallEvent CalendarWatchlistPodcastMore Pricing Ask AgentReal-time News WatchlistMarketEarnings CallEvent CalendarPodcast CSPC Pharmaceutical's RSV Vaccine Cleared by U.S. FDA to Begin Clinical Trials in the United States 2026-08-25 14:35 (GMT+0) Add to Google Preferred Sources CSPC Pharmaceutical Group (01093.HK) announced that its independently developed recombinant respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) vaccine, SYS6057, has received approval from the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) to commence clinical trials in the United States. The vaccine is the group's second recombinant protein vaccine to receive clinical trial clearance, composed of an RSV prefusion F protein antigen and the company's proprietary second-generation nanoparticle adjuvant system. The clinical indication targets adults aged 60 and older for the prevention of RSV-induced lower respiratory tract disease. SYS6057 had previously received approval from China's National Medical Products Administration on July 30, 2026, to conduct clinical trials in China. Market observers note that the RSV vaccine market is highly competitive, and CSPC's differentiated adjuvant technology could establish a competitive edge, though the lengthy clinical development timeline means near-term revenue contribution will be limited. Key Elements CSPC Pharmaceutical GroupSYS6057U.S. Food and Drug AdministrationChina's National Medical Products AdministrationRespiratory Syncytial VirusRecombinant Protein VaccineSecond-Generation Nanoparticle Adjuvant CSPC Pharmaceutical Group (01093.HK) announced that its independently developed Class 1 preventive biologic product, the "Recombinant Respiratory Syncytial Virus Vaccine (SYS6057)," has received approval from the U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) to conduct clinical trials within the United States. This vaccine marks CSPC's second recombinant protein vaccine product to obtain clinical trial clearance, signaling the group's vaccine R&D pipeline has formally entered the international multi-center development stage. According to the company's announcement, the core composition of SYS6057 includes a respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) prefusion F protein (Pre-F) antigen, along with the group's independently developed second-generation nanoparticle adjuvant system. Compared with previous products, this vaccine's adjuvant employs a different nanoparticle carrier, exhibiting unique physicochemical properties and immunogenic activity. Through optimization of the adjuvant system, SYS6057 is capable of simultaneously inducing high-titer neutralizing antibodies against RSV and robust cellular immune responses, offering distinctive immunoprotective advantages. Preclinical study data indicate the product demonstrates favorable safety and efficacy profiles. The clinical indication approved in the United States targets adults aged 60 and older for the prevention of RSV-induced lower respiratory tract disease (LRTD). Prior to this, SYS6057 had already received approval from China's National Medical Products Administration (NMPA) on July 30, 2026, to conduct clinical trials in China. With the FDA's clearance, the vaccine has now formally entered a dual-filing stage with simultaneous clinical advancement in both China and the United States. CSPC Pharmaceutical Group has been actively building out its vaccine and biologics pipeline in recent years, with recombinant protein vaccines serving as a key technology platform. The advancement of SYS6057 lays the foundation for the group's subsequent recombinant protein vaccine and adjuvant product development. Industry observers note that competition in the RSV vaccine market has intensified in recent years, with major international players having already launched related products. However, unmet preventive needs remain for the elderly population, and CSPC's entry at this juncture presents an opportunity to establish a competitive advantage through differentiated adjuvant technology. From a financial perspective, CSPC's performance in the Hong Kong stock market has been relatively stable, with a short-selling ratio of approximately 10.1%, indicating some divergence in market views on its near-term trajectory. Nevertheless, news of a new drug receiving clinical trial clearance in the United States is typically viewed as a positive signal of R&D capability, helping to bolster investor confidence in the company's medium-to-long-term growth momentum. Notably, the trend of Chinese biotech companies filing new drug clinical trial applications in the United States continues to accelerate. Just one day before CSPC's announcement, Baoji Pharmaceutical-B (02659.HK) also disclosed that its independently developed anti-HER2 bispecific antibody subcutaneous formulation, KJ015 injection, received Investigational New Drug (IND) clearance from the U.S. FDA on August 21, 2026. KJ015 similarly employs a dual-filing strategy in China and the United States, having previously obtained IND approval from China's NMPA in December 2024 and initiated a Phase I clinical trial in China in June 2025. Multiple Chinese biotech companies obtaining U.S. FDA clinical clearances within a similar timeframe reflects the accelerating pace of internationalization among Chinese innovative drug developers. For CSPC Pharmaceutical Group, the initiation of SYS6057 clinical trials in the United States represents not only the advancement of a single product but also preliminary recognition by international regulators of the group's technology export capabilities on its vaccine adjuvant platform. The performance of subsequent clinical data will be the key determinant of whether this vaccine can break through in the highly competitive RSV market. Market analysts note that the clinical development cycle for RSV vaccines is relatively lengthy, typically requiring several years from Phase I to Phase III, and investors should not overestimate near-term revenue contributions. However, from a pipeline valuation perspective, if SYS6057 progresses smoothly, it will open up growth opportunities for CSPC's vaccine business and further strengthen the group's competitive position in the biologics sector. 孕期疫苗(RSV):母体免疫如何帮助保护新生儿 - 美国感染性疾病基金会

Vaccines During Pregnancy: How Maternal Immunization Helps Protect Your Newborn - National Foundation for Infectious Diseases
新闻简报美国感染性疾病基金会(NFID)于2026年8月20日发布的文章强调孕期免疫在保护新生儿免受感染性疾病(包括疫苗(RSV))的重要性。文章解释了孕期疫苗如何为婴儿提供免疫力,降低其罹患RSV等疾病重症的风险。该文还涉及麻疹、HPV及脑膜炎球菌病等其他感染性疾病,强调免疫接种在公共卫生中的更广泛作用。文章未报告与RSV相关的任何具体暴发或病例数,但强调了疫苗接种在预防疾病传播及确保健康公平中的重要性。NFID还提及即将举办的活动,包括返校免疫讨论及颁奖晚宴,以提升对疫苗的认知与教育。该内容为一般性科普文章,未提供暴发应对或调查的具体细节。

K-Bio Firms Race to Enter $28 Billion RSV Prevention Market - Seoul Economic Daily

K-Bio Firms Race to Enter $28 Billion RSV Prevention Market - Seoul Economic Daily
新闻简报The news report from Seoul Economic Daily, published on August 24, 2026, highlights the growing competition among Korean biopharmaceutical firms to enter the $28 billion RSV prevention market. It notes that Pfizer's RSV vaccine Abrysvo has recently received approval from the Ministry of Food and Drug Safety and is set to launch. Domestic companies such as SK Bioscience and EuBiologics are also advancing their clinical trials for RSV prevention products. The global RSV prevention market, which includes vaccines and monoclonal antibodies, is expected to grow significantly, reaching $28.39 billion by 2030 with a compound annual growth rate of 42.2%. RSV, a respiratory infection, can cause severe conditions like bronchiolitis and pneumonia in infants and older adults with weakened immune systems. The report suggests that the market's expansion may lead to price competition as new entrants prepare for the RSV season. However, it does not provide specific details on the current outbreak status or public health responses to RSV in South Korea.

报道聚焦韩国,RSV主要在每年10月至次年3月流行,并涉及SK生物科学、欧必素生物等本土企业。

K-Bio Firms Race to Enter $28 Billion RSV Prevention Market - Seoul Economic Daily

News Brief
The news report from Seoul Economic Daily, published on August 24, 2026, highlights the growing competition among Korean biopharmaceutical firms to enter the $28 billion RSV prevention market. Skip to main content Seoul Economic Daily Markets Property Finance Business Technology Politics Society Culture Sports International Opinion AI KEY Seoul Economic Daily August 26, 2026 (Wed) KSTSign In Seoul Economic Daily Korea's Leading Business News Since 1960 한국어 Markets Property Finance Business Technology Politics Society Culture Sports International Opinion AI KEY KOSPI6,742.74▲+0.68% KOSDAQ827.15▲+3.14% USD/KRW1,382.36▲+0.02% EUR/KRW1,613.70▲+0.14% KOSPI6,742.74▲+0.68% KOSDAQ827.15▲+3.14% USD/KRW1,382.36▲+0.02% EUR/KRW1,613.70▲+0.14% KOSPI6,742.74▲+0.68% KOSDAQ827.15▲+3.14% USD/KRW1,382.36▲+0.02% EUR/KRW1,613.70▲+0.14% KOSPI6,742.74▲+0.68% KOSDAQ827.15▲+3.14% USD/KRW1,382.36▲+0.02% EUR/KRW1,613.70▲+0.14% Menu Saved ArticlesNewslettersAI KEYSettings K-Bio Firms Race to Enter $28 Billion RSV Prevention Market Competition Heats Up in RSV Prevention Market Pfizer and MSD Join In as Domestic Rivalry Intensifies RSV Vaccine and Antibody Market to Grow 42.2% Annually SK Bioscience and EuBiologics Accelerate Clinical Development Culture| Published 2026.08.24. 07:00:05 | By An Kyung-jinrealglasses@sedaily.com |Summary|Save AAA Clipart Korea The global market for preventing respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) is projected to reach 42 trillion won ($28 billion) by 2030, and pharmaceutical and biotech companies are ramping up their competition for market leadership. Korea Pfizer's RSV vaccine Abrysvo recently won approval from the Ministry of Food and Drug Safety and is set to launch, industry sources said on the 23rd. RSV is a respiratory infection that circulates in South Korea mainly between October and March. In infants and older adults with weaker immune systems, it can lead to severe respiratory conditions such as bronchiolitis and pneumonia. Prevention options had been limited, but the market has grown rapidly in recent years as vaccines and monoclonal antibody injections have come to market in succession. According to market research firm Grand View Research, the global RSV prevention market, including vaccines and monoclonal antibodies, is expected to grow from $3.51 billion in 2024 to $28.39 billion (about 42.2 trillion won) by 2030. That represents a compound annual growth rate of 42.2%. RSV prevention is divided into vaccines and monoclonal antibodies, depending on how immunity is formed. Vaccines take an active immunization approach, delivering an antigen to prompt the body to produce its own antibodies, while monoclonal antibodies use passive immunization, directly delivering ready-made antibodies from an external source. Because protection can be expected immediately after administration, they are useful for infants whose immune systems are not yet fully developed. In the domestic adult RSV vaccine market, GSK's Arexvy was the first to enter, followed by Moderna's mRNA vaccine mResvia and Abrysvo, which have both won approval, forming a three-way race. In infant prevention, Sanofi's long-acting monoclonal antibody Beyfortus took an early lead in the market, and Korea MSD's Enflonsia won approval this year. With later entrants expected to launch ahead of the RSV season, whether this will lead to price cuts is drawing attention. Domestic companies, noting the growth potential of the global RSV prevention market, are also moving quickly to secure products. SK Bioscience (302440.KS), which handles domestic sales of Beyfortus, is pursuing a global Phase 1 trial of RSM01, a long-acting antibody candidate for infants. EuBiologics (206650.KQ) recently received the results report from a domestic Phase 1 trial of its RSV prevention vaccine EuRSV and is preparing for Phase 2. 尼塞韦单抗 在婴儿首个呼吸道合胞病毒季节中减少抗生素使用——Medscape

Nirsevimab Cuts Antibiotic Use for ARTIs in Infants - Medscape
新闻简报2026年8月6日发表于《临床感染病杂志》的一项研究发现,尼塞韦单抗 的使用与婴儿在首个呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)季节期间的抗生素处方量减少相关。该研究在宾夕法尼亚州和新泽西州的一个儿科护理网络中进行,涉及2023-2024和2024-2025 RSV季节期间于4月1日至9月30日期间出生的15,341名婴儿(平均年龄3.5个月)。该研究为模拟目标试验,显示门诊急性呼吸道感染(ARTIs)抗生素使用减少14.4%,门诊毛细支气管炎抗生素使用减少40.3%,RSV相关住院减少69.4%。研究结果表明,尼塞韦单抗 可能有助于应对婴儿RSV感染中的抗生素过度使用问题。然而,该研究的回顾性设计及对电子健康记录的依赖可能引入分类错误风险。此外,研究无法调整未测量因素(如医疗服务使用)对尼塞韦单抗 给药的影响。研究结果凸显了尼塞韦单抗 作为二级工具在减少抗生素使用方面的潜力,但仍需进一步研究以在更广泛人群中确认其有效性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年8月6日发表,随访期为6个月。RSV季节分为流行前期(10月-11月)、流行高峰期(12月-1月)和流行后期(2月-3月)。
地点与对象
该研究在宾夕法尼亚州和新泽西州的一个儿科护理网络中进行,涉及2023-2024和2024-2025 RSV季节期间于4月1日至9月30日期间出生的15,341名婴儿(平均年龄3.5个月)。
事件
尼塞韦单抗 的使用与婴儿在首个呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)季节期间的抗生素处方量减少相关,其中急性呼吸道感染(ARTIs)抗生素使用减少超过14%,毛细支气管炎抗生素使用减少超过40%,RSV相关住院减少近70%。该研究为模拟目标试验。
规模、影响与风险
在6个月随访期内,尼塞韦单抗 给药与门诊急性呼吸道感染(ARTIs)抗生素处方量减少14.4%相关。同时,其还使门诊毛细支气管炎抗生素使用减少40.3%,RSV相关住院减少69.4%。研究结果凸显了其在减少抗生素过度使用方面的潜在作用。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究仅限于宾夕法尼亚州和新泽西州的一个儿科护理网络,可能影响其全国普遍适用性。回顾性设计依赖电子健康记录数据,可能导致暴露和结局的分类错误。研究无法调整未测量因素(如医疗服务使用),这些因素可能影响接受尼塞韦单抗 的机会。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nirsevimab Cuts Antibiotic Use for ARTIs in Infants - Medscape

News Brief
A study published in Clinical Infectious Diseases on August 6, 2026, found that nirsevimab use was associated with reduced antibiotic prescribing among infants during their first RSV season. The research, conducted in a pediatric care network across Pennsylvania and New Jersey, involved 15,341 infants (mean age, 3.5 months) born between April 1 and September 30 during the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 RSV seasons. The study, an emulated target trial, showed a 14.4% reduction in antibiotic use for outpatient acute respiratory tract infections (ARTIs), a 40.3% reduction for outpatient bronchiolitis, and a 69.4% reduction for RSV-related hospitalizations. These findings suggest nirsevimab could help combat antibiotic overuse in infants with RSV infections. However, the study's retrospective design and reliance on electronic health records may introduce misclassification risks. Additionally, the study could not adjust for unmeasured factors like healthcare use, which might have influenced nirsevimab administration. The results highlight the potential of nirsevimab as a secondary tool in reducing antibiotic use, but further research is needed to confirm its effectiveness in broader populations.
Original source text (English)
Medscape AI News & Perspective Tools & Reference CME/CE More NEW! Ask complex medical questions and find the latest clinical guidance for drugs & conditions with Medscape AI× EN RegisterLog In AI Mode Medscape Medical NewsMedBrief Medbrief Nirsevimab Cuts Antibiotic Use in Infants With Respiratory Infections During RSV Season Edited by Gargi Mukherjee August 24, 2026Clin Infect Dis. 2026;doi:10.1093/cid/ciag406 0 Add to Email Alerts TOPLINE In an emulated target trial, nirsevimab use was associated with reduced antibiotic prescribing among infants during their first respiratory syncytial virus (RSV) season, with reductions of more than 14% for acute respiratory tract infections (ARTIs), more than 40% for bronchiolitis, and nearly 70% for RSV-related hospitalizations. METHODOLOGY Researchers emulated a target trial using electronic health record data from the Children’s Hospital of Philadelphia Primary Care Network to assess whether nirsevimab reduced antibiotic use for ARTIs in outpatient and inpatient settings. They included 15,341 infants (mean age, 3.5 months) born between April 1 and September 30 during the 2023-2024 and 2024-2025 RSV seasons with at least one primary care visit within 14 days of birth. RSV season was defined by the month when each emulated trial began and categorized as prepeak (October-November), peak (December-January), or postpeak (February-March). Researchers used a sequential trial design, with the study period divided into six monthly intervals, and compared outcomes among infants who received nirsevimab (n = 7413) and those who did not (n = 7928). Primary outcomes included time to first antibiotic prescription for outpatient ARTIs, outpatient bronchiolitis encounters, and RSV-related hospitalizations; nirsevimab effectiveness was calculated as 1 minus the risk ratio. TAKEAWAY At 6 months of follow-up, nirsevimab administration was associated with a 14.4% reduction (95% CI, 7.8%-20.6%) in antibiotic prescribing for outpatient ARTIs compared with no nirsevimab. Nirsevimab reduced antibiotic prescribing by 40.3% for outpatient bronchiolitis (95% CI, 25.1%-52.5%) and by 69.4% for RSV-related hospitalizations (95% CI, 4.8%-90.1%). In exploratory secondary analyses, nirsevimab was associated with a 26.8% reduction (95% CI, 20.8%-32.4%) in antibiotic prescribing for acute otitis media and a 22.5% reduction (95% CI, 12.6%-31.3%) for viral respiratory infections. IN PRACTICE “Nirsevimab administration reduced antibiotic use for ARTIs across settings, highlighting its secondary importance as an effective tool for combating antibiotic overuse,” the authors wrote. “Increasing currently suboptimal rates of nirsevimab coverage among infants in their first year of life should be a priority not only to prevent the morbidity associated with RSV bronchiolitis but also to supplement existing antibiotic stewardship efforts,” they concluded. SOURCE This study was led by Mahaa M. Ahmed, MS, Children’s Hospital of Philadelphia, and Torsten Joerger, MD, University of Pennsylvania Perelman School of Medicine and Children’s Hospital of Philadelphia, both in Philadelphia. It was published online on August 6, 2026, in Clinical Infectious Diseases. LIMITATIONS The study was limited to a pediatric care network across Pennsylvania and New Jersey, which may limit its national generalizability. The retrospective design relied on data recorded in the electronic health record, creating the potential for misclassification of exposures and outcomes. The study could not adjust for unmeasured factors, such as healthcare use, which may have influenced the chances of receiving nirsevimab as well as having a medically attended visit. DISCLOSURES This research received support from the Infectious Diseases Society of America’s Grants for Emerging Researchers/Clinicians Mentorship program. The authors disclosed no relevant conflicts of interest. SUGGESTED FOR YOU This article was created using several editorial tools, including AI, as part of the process. Human editors reviewed this content before publication. 家庭如何为呼吸道病毒季做准备? - 《新闻报》

How can families prepare for respiratory-virus season? - The News Journal

How can families prepare for respiratory-virus season? - The News Journal

News Brief
The article from The News Journal, published on August 25, 2026, discusses how families in the Mid-Atlantic region can prepare for the upcoming respiratory-virus season. Health experts recommend measures such as flu shots, RSV awareness, handwashing, and everyday hygiene habits to reduce the spread of illness and protect vulnerable family members. The guidance emphasizes the importance of proactive steps to stay healthy during the fall and winter months when respiratory viruses like RSV are more prevalent. The article does not report any confirmed outbreaks or specific cases but highlights general prevention strategies. The information is based on recommendations from healthcare organizations and is presented as a preventive measure rather than an outbreak report.

皮尔斯县呼吸道感染病例上升——塔科马周报

Respiratory infections increasing in Pierce County - TacomaWeekly
新闻简报The article from Tacoma Weekly, published on August 20, 2026, reports an increase in respiratory infections, including Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV), in Pierce County. The Tacoma-Pierce County Health Department notes that RSV is circulating and contributing to a rise in emergency room visits at Mary Bridge Children’s Hospital. The CDC reports that RSV typically infects nearly all children by age 2 and can cause severe illness in infants, especially premature babies, and in adults with certain health conditions. It also states that 1-2% of children under 6 months infected with RSV may require hospitalization. The article emphasizes prevention measures such as staying home when sick, covering coughs and sneezes, frequent handwashing, and wearing masks around high-risk individuals. Additionally, it highlights the availability of RSV prevention injections and updated COVID-19 boosters for those eligible, urging the public to take precautions this respiratory season.
查看新闻五要素
时间
这篇文章发布于2026-08-20。证据中未明确说明事件日期,但文中提到流感季节已经开始且呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)病例正在增加,表明该事件正在进行中。
地点与对象
文章涉及皮尔斯县,具体提及塔科马及玛丽桥儿童医院。文中重点关注5岁以下儿童,尤其是2岁以下婴幼儿,以及65岁及以上成人、孕妇及哮喘、糖尿病、心脏病等基础疾病患者等高风险人群。
事件
皮尔斯县呼吸道感染病例(包括呼吸道合胞病毒)正在上升,由塔科马-皮尔斯县卫生部门报告。文章指出,呼吸道合胞病毒病例正在增加,并导致玛丽桥儿童医院急诊就诊人数上升。
规模、影响与风险
文章指出,呼吸道合胞病毒几乎会感染所有2岁以下儿童,并可导致婴儿(尤其是早产儿)和患有某些基础疾病的成人出现严重疾病。CDC数据显示,6个月以下感染呼吸道合胞病毒的儿童中,1-2%可能需要住院治疗。
应对、状态与不确定性
当文章讨论预防措施时,包括生病时待在家中、咳嗽和打喷嚏时遮掩、洗手、在高风险人群周围佩戴口罩以及限制在托儿中心的时间。文章还提到,符合条件的人群可获得呼吸道合胞病毒预防注射和更新的COVID-19加强针。

Respiratory infections increasing in Pierce County - TacomaWeekly

News Brief
The article from Tacoma Weekly, published on August 20, 2026, reports an increase in respiratory infections, including Respiratory Syncytial Virus (RSV), in Pierce County. The Tacoma-Pierce County Health Department notes that RSV is circulating and contributing to a rise in emergency room visits at Mary Bridge Children’s Hospital. The CDC reports that RSV typically infects nearly all children by age 2 and can cause severe illness in infants, especially premature babies, and in adults with certain health conditions. Original source text (English)
For nearly 40 years, the Tacoma Weekly has been a source of information about local events, the people, and the places of our city – news that residents can’t get anywhere else. Our job is to serve the community, to tell the stories that people want to read to be better informed, and to bring a sense of unity to Tacoma’s diverse human landscape. Publishing a community newspaper brings with it a lot of responsibilities, the first of which is to be responsible to the people we serve – all people. This is why t he Tacoma Weekly print edition and website are available free to everyone from all backgrounds and incomes. Inclusion must be intentional, consistent, and for the right reasons, which starts with the ethics of the people producing the newspaper. We find it an honor and a blessing to embrace all cultures because it makes us better people. Learning is a never-ending outcome of engaging with folks from all walks of life. Over these past decades of publishing, we have grown to see ourselves as a co-op in that we would not be here were it not for the very people who give us something to write about and read about. The people of our city are the true owners of the Tacoma Weekly for we wouldn’t exist without them. They bring us the story ideas and leads, businesses owners and organization leaders invest their advertising dollar with us, and they pick up the paper and read it. Our mission is the same today as it was when our first issue came off the press. We tell Tacoma’s stories. It has been tough for the newspaper industry going back to when the world wide web became the "information superhighway” and print media gradually reduced in its wake. Things got even tougher under the Trump administration with trigger words like "fake news” attempting to set up a conflict in people’s minds about the integrity of reporters and what is and isn’t truthful in journalism. Then COVID hit, taking out small businesses across the country and putting the Tacoma Weekly at risk of not being able to continue printing. But this is where the critics underestimated the public’s intelligence. The loyalty of our readers and advertisers has not swayed even during these turbulent times, and for that we say thank you. Respiratory infections increasing in Pierce County By Nigel Turner Tacoma-Pierce County Health Department COVID-19 is still circulating in Pierce County. Flu season has begun. And now, Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) report an increase in cases of Respiratory Syncytial Virus Infection (RSV). According to healthcare providers at Mary Bridge Children’s Hospital in Tacoma, the rise in RSV cases is also contributing to an increase in emergency room visits. The hospital created a tool to help you decide if you need to take your child to the ER, urgent care, or treat at home. Using the hospital only when you truly need it helps keep this important resource available for emergencies, surgeries, and for those most vulnerable to severe illness. What is RSV? RSV infects nearly all children by the time they turn 2. It usually causes only mild cold-like symptoms in older children and adults but can cause severe illness in infants, especially premature babies, those with certain heart or lung diseases, and older adults. It is the most common cause of severe lung infections in children younger than 1 in the U.S. About 58,000 U.S. children under 5 are hospitalized with RSV each year. CDC says 1-2% of children under 6 months who are infected with RSV may need to be hospitalized. RSV can spread when: · Someone who has RSV coughs or sneezes. · You get virus droplets from a cough or sneeze in your eyes, nose, or mouth. · You have direct contact with the virus, like kissing the face of a child with RSV. · You touch a surface that has the virus on it, like a doorknob, and then touch your face before washing your hands. People infected with RSV are usually contagious for 3 to 8 days and may become contagious a day or 2 before they start showing signs of illness. Simple steps to protect your family. If your baby is at high risk for RSV infection, you can ask your healthcare provider if your baby is eligible for an injection that could prevent it. You can also help limit the spread of RSV just like you’ve done for COVID-19 the past few years and the flu before that. Especially if you are around young children or older adults, take these steps: · Stay home if you’re sick. · Cover your coughs and sneezes. · Wash your hands often. · Consider wearing a mask around those most at risk. · Limit the time you spend in childcare centers or other potentially contagious settings. Boost your protection. Everyone should take precautions this respiratory season. But some groups are especially at risk for severe illness: · Children under 5 years (especially under 2 years). · Adults 65 years or older. · Those who are pregnant. · Anyone living with a health condition like asthma, diabetes or heart disease. You can find more opportunities at tpchd.org/kidsvax, and learn more about the flu at tpchd.org/flu. Now is also the time to get your updated COVID-19 booster. Bivalent Pfizer and Moderna boosters protect against both the original COVID-19 strain and omicron variants BA.4 and BA.5. They provide the best protection against severe illness. Everyone 5 and older is eligible to get an updated booster 2 months after your last booster dose or primary series of vaccines. We also have Novavax boosters for people 18 and older who can’t or don’t want to get an mRNA vaccine. Find your dose today at tpchd.org/vaxtothefuture. Help us stay safe this fall. We’ll keep you posted on ways you and your family can stay safe this fall. Sign up for blog updates and other notifications and follow us on Facebook, Twitter and Instagram to get our latest reliable information about respiratory infections and other important public health topics. Nigel Turner leads the work at TPCHD to help keep your family safe from communicable diseases

尽管对疫苗指导意见存在混淆,Phoebe表示医疗建议未改变 - Phoebe Putney医疗系统

Despite Confusion over Vaccine Guidance Phoebe Says the Medical Recommendation Hasn’t Changed - Phoebe Putney Health System
新闻简报2026年8月24日,Phoebe Putney医疗系统重申其针对免疫接种时间表的医疗建议保持不变,强调疫苗在预防呼吸道合胞病毒(RSV)、麻疹等疾病中的重要性。尽管联邦政府就疫苗接种要求发布了新的指导意见,该医疗系统表示,美国儿科学会等机构继续支持标准儿童免疫接种时间表,其中包括针对RSV、麻疹及其他疾病的疫苗。Phoebe医疗系统初级保健医疗主任Derek Heard博士指出,尽管脊髓灰质炎在美国已根除,但其在全球仍有传播,因此疫苗接种仍有必要。他还警告称,错过疫苗可能带来风险,并引用了佐治亚州近期麻疹病例及可能出现的脑炎或肺炎等并发症。该医疗系统将免疫接种视为社区共同责任,特别是在西南佐治亚地区,慢性病如糖尿病和慢性阻塞性肺病(COPD)的患病率较高。Phoebe敦促居民利用国家免疫宣传月,确保自己和家人完成所有推荐的疫苗接种。该医疗系统强调,这些建议基于数十年的医学证据和全球健康数据。
